## Meta

### **1. Overall Meta Summary**

  - **Total Spend:** RM 23,192.54
  - **Total Leads:** 265
  - **Blended CPL:** RM 87.52
  - **Key Success:** Instant Form campaigns are highly efficient (RM 45.51 CPL).
  - **Key Challenge:** Website Conversion campaigns, while driving volume, have a high CPL (RM 97.77). Engagement-focused campaigns are inefficient (CPL \> RM 100).

### **1. Executive Summary**

  - **Total Spend:** RM 23,192.54
  - **Total Leads:** 265
  - **Blended CPL:** RM 87.52
  - **Key Success:** Instant Form campaigns are highly efficient (RM 45.51 average CPL).
  - **Key Challenge:** Website Conversion campaigns, while driving volume, have a high CPL (RM 97.77 average CPL). Engagement-focused campaigns are inefficient (CPL \> RM 100).
  - **Top 3 Recommendations:**
      
    1.  Increase budget for high-performing Instant Form campaigns.
    2.  Optimize or pause high-CPL Website Conversion campaigns.
    3.  Pause all MOF Engagement campaigns (Message Conversations & LPV) due to poor CPL.

#### **Marketing Insights & Analysis**

  - **Retargeting is the Clear Winner:** Social media or website visitors audiences are consistently top performing. These users have prior context and are more cost-effective to convert, especially for lower-friction Instant Form objectives.  
    ★ This suggests running more TOF campaigns to increase the pool of users in these audience lists.
  - **Specific Interests Outperform Broad Stacks:** The Areas of study stack (RM 59.34 CPL) performed significantly better than generic Int stack (RM 142.33 & RM 358.12 CPLs). This indicates that specific, relevant interest targeting is crucial for Website Conversion campaigns.
  - **Video Viewers Are Not Converting:** The “Video Viewers" audience are consistently underperforming.  
    ★ This is likely because the videos in use to create the lists are outdated.

## Meta  
  

### **Meta Performance Report**

#### **1. Executive Summary**

  - **Total Spend:** RM 21,441.28
  - **Total Leads:** 244
  - **Blended CPL:** RM 88

Meta's performance is a story of two distinct strategies: the **Instant Form** campaigns are operating with high efficiency, while the **Website Conversion** campaigns are significantly driving up the overall CPL.   
Due to higher resistance for users to submit leads via website compared to instant forms, we will continue shifting budget towards instant forms slowly.

  

#### **2. Performance Analysis**

**Instant Form Campaigns**

|  |  |  |  |
| :-: | :-: | :-: | :-: |
| Campaign Name | Total Leads | Total Cost (RM) | CPL (RM) |
| Audaura/ FB/ BOF/ Lead Generation/ Instant Form/ A2/ T2/ ABO | 18 | 568.41 | 31.58 |
| Audaura/ FB/ BOF/ Lead Generation/ Instant Form/ A1/ T1a+T1b/ ABO | 26 | 1,021.07 | 39.27 |
| Audaura/ FB/ BOF/ Lead Generation/ Instant Form/ A2/ T1a+T1b/ ABO | 30 | 1,224.08 | 40.80 |
| Audaura/ FB/ BOF/ Lead Generation/ Instant Form/ A1/ T2 ABO | 19 | 811.13 | 42.69 |

**Insight:** All Instant Form campaigns are performing exceptionally well, delivering a consistent CPL.

**Website Conversion & Other Campaigns**

|  |  |  |  |
| :-: | :-: | :-: | :-: |
| Campaign Name | Total Leads | Total Cost (RM) | CPL (RM) |
| Audaura/ FB/ BOF/ Lead Generation/ Website Conversion/ A2/ T1a+T1b+T2/ ABO | 60 | 6,674.75 | 111.25 |
| Audaura/ FB/ BOF/ Lead Generation/ Website Conversion/ A1/ T1a+T1b+T2/ ABO | 72 | 8,759.53 | 121.66 |

**Insight:** The Website Conversion campaigns are more expensive than our Instant Form campaigns. This indicates a significant drop-off or friction point on the website landing page.

#### **3. Ad Set Level Performance Analysis**

**Top 3 Performing Ad Sets (by CPL)**

|  |  |  |  |
| :-: | :-: | :-: | :-: |
| AdSet Name | Total Leads | Total Cost (RM) | CPL (RM) |
| A2/ T2/ fm/ 1850/ Social Media Engagers - 120/ BM/ Max Conversion Leads | 9 | 226.60 | 25.18 |
| A2/ T2/ fm/ 1850/ Unitar Website Visitors - 120/ BM/ Max Conversion Leads | 8 | 228.98 | 28.62 |
| A1/ T2/ fm/ 1850/ Int Level of study stack/ BM/ Max Conversion Leads | 13 | 564.46 | 43.42 |

**Insight:** Retargeting audiences (Social Media Engagers, Website Visitors) are delivering the most efficient leads, confirming the value of engaging with a warm audience.

**Worst 3 Performing Ad Sets (by CPL)**

|  |  |  |  |
| :-: | :-: | :-: | :-: |
| AdSet Name | Total Leads | Total Cost (RM) | CPL (RM) |
| A1/ T1a+T1b/ fm/ 1850/ Int generic stack/ EN/ Max Conversion Leads | 4 | 596.20 | 149.05 |
| A1/ T1a+T1b/ fm/ 1850/ Int Level of study stack/ EN/ Max Conversion Leads | 3 | 591.79 | 197.26 |
| A1/ T1a+T1b/ fm/ 1850/ Broad/ EN/ Max Conversion Leads | 2 | 734.63 | 367.32 |

**Insight:** Broad targeting and interest-based stacks are proving to be highly inefficient and are not delivering a positive return on investment.

#### **4. Ad Level (Creative) Performance Analysis: Lead Generation Focus**

**Top 3 Performing Ads (by CPL)**

|  |  |  |  |
| :-: | :-: | :-: | :-: |
| Ad Name | Total Leads | Total Cost (RM) | CPL (RM) |
| a2\\\_uuckl\\\_diploma\\\_businessmanagement\\\_static\\\_v1\\\_en | 8 | 145.88 | 18.24 |
| a1\\\_generic\\\_foundation\\\_static\\\_v1\\\_bm\\\_nootp | 88 | 3,346.40 | 38.03 |

**Insight:** Creatives focused on specific programs like Business Management and Foundation studies are performing very well. The "nootp" creative, in particular, is a high-volume winner.

**Worst 3 Performing Ads (by CPL)**

|  |  |  |  |
| :-: | :-: | :-: | :-: |
| Ad Name | Total Leads | Total Cost (RM) | CPL (RM) |
| a2\\\_maincampus\\\_bachelors\\\_internationalbusiness\\\_static\\\_v1\\\_en | 0 | 41.46 | NaN |
| a0\\\_generic\\\_bachelors\\\_static\\\_v1\\\_en | 0 | 84.40 | NaN |
| a2\\\_uuckl\\\_diploma\\\_diplomainearlychildhoodeducation\\\_static\\\_v1\\\_en | 0 | 169.50 | NaN |

**Insight:** Several static image ads failed to generate any leads despite their spend. These creatives will be paused immediately to prevent further wasted budget.

#### **5. Action Steps**

1.  **Pause Inefficient Campaigns:** Immediately pause the two "Website Conversion" campaigns and the "Message Conversations" campaign. This will stop the high-CPL spending and preserve budget.
2.  **Consolidate Budget to Instant Forms:** Reallocate the entire budget from the paused campaigns to the four high-performing "Instant Form" campaigns. This will maximize lead volume at the proven CPL of \~RM 39.
3.  **Refine Ad Set Targeting:** Pause the "Broad" and "Interest Stack" ad sets. Reallocate their budgets to the top-performing retargeting ad sets (Social Media Engagers, Website Visitors).
4.  **Optimize Creatives:** Pause all ads that generated zero leads. Create new ad variations based on the successful "Business Management" and "Foundation" creatives to test against the current winners.

#### **5. Action Steps**

1.  **Harvest and Scale Winning Keywords:** Create new, single-keyword-ad-groups (SKAGs) for the top-performing keywords ("wawasan open university", "private university application", etc.). Allocate a dedicated test budget to these new campaigns to see if their efficiency can be maintained at a higher volume.
2.  **Audit PMax Asset Groups:** Conduct a full audit of all Performance Max asset groups. Pause any asset groups with a CPL over RM 150 and re-upload the top-performing video creatives from TikTok and Meta as new assets to improve creative relevance.

# 7th July - 13th July  

# **7th July - 13th July****  
  
**

## **Overall Executive Summary****  
****  
**This week, we invested a total of **RM 178,921** across all digital platforms, generating **2,385 leads** at a blended Cost Per Lead (CPL) of **RM75**.

### **Platform Performance Overview**

|  |  |  |  |
| :-: | :-: | :-: | :-: |
| \#\#\# \*\*Platform\*\* | \#\#\# \*\*Spend (RM)\*\* | \#\#\# \*\*Leads\*\* | \#\#\# \*\*CPL (RM)\*\* |

The combined spend on core campaigns across Meta and TikTok was **RM 22,004.87**, generating **420** leads and conversions. This results in a blended CPL of **RM 52.39**.

  - ★ **Biggest Win:** Google's competitor-focused search campaigns are the most efficient tactic across all platforms, acquiring conversions for just **RM 31.60**. This is a highly profitable, scalable strategy.
  - ★ **Top 3 Action Items:**
      
    1.  **Re-evaluate TikTok's Role:** With the hyper-efficient TOF campaigns excluded, TikTok's core lead-gen CPL is not competitive, unless new video content is made available asap. We must decide whether to optimize these campaigns or scale down.
    2.  **Double Down on Google Competitor Campaigns:** The most efficient source of leads is Google Search campaigns targeting competitors. This budget should be protected and scaled.
    3.  **Optimize Meta by Objective:** The Website Conversion campaigns on Meta are driving the most volume, but Instant Form campaigns are slightly more CPL-efficient. A budget shift towards Instant Forms could improve overall Meta efficiency.
          
          - *\*OTP validation\**
          - *\*Higher quality instant forms\**

3.  **Recommended Action:** Pause all keywords with a CPL above RM 250 in the Search\_Competitor campaign that have not converted in the last 7 days.

3.  **Scale 'SPM' Campaigns:** The 'spm' ad groups ('t1a\_bm\_spm' and 't2\_bm\_spm') are delivering a high volume of conversions at a good CPL. Test increasing the budget for these campaigns to capture more of this efficient volume.
4.  **Refine Competitor & Generic Strategy:** Keywords like 'unikl' show that competitor bidding can be efficient. Test expanding the list of competitor keywords. For expensive generic keywords like 'accounting course', create more specific ad groups with tighter themes (e.g., "part-time accounting degree") to improve relevance and lower CPL.

| Audaura/ FB/ BOF/ Lead Generation/ Instant Form/ A1/ T1a+T1b/ ABO | 8,578 | 278 | 31 |   |
| Audaura/ FB/ BOF/ Lead Generation/ Instant Form/ A2/ T1a+T1b/ ABO | 8,671 | 213 | 41 |   |
| Audaura/ FB/ BOF/ Lead Generation/ Instant Form/ A2/ T2/ ABO | 5,471 | 122 | 45 |   |
| Audaura/ FB/ BOF/ Lead Generation/ Instant Form/ A1/ T2 ABO | 7,197 | 153 | 47 |   |

*Insight: Prospecting Instant Form campaigns are the clear winner, with an average CPL below RM 40 and top performers operating under RM 31.*

  

#### **Action Steps**

1.  **Recommended Action:** Shift \~20% of the budget (\~RM 13,000) from the two Website Conversion campaigns to the top two Instant Form campaigns.
      
      - **Expected Outcome:** Generate an estimated 120-150 additional leads for the same cost, lowering the blended Meta CPL by 15-20%.
2.  Start LEAD GEN focused video ads to improve funnel overall.
3.  Test creatives with different marketing focus e.g listed on Cycle 3 Focus Areas sheet.

### **Ad Set Level Performance Insight**

The BM retargeting audiences in the A2 are top performers.   
However, the EN Social Media Engagers and BM Website Visitors in the A2/T1b structure are among the worst.  
Meaning that the T1b is failing to convert even warm audiences efficiently. This may be mitigated by running TOF campaigns in T1b but that is not viable due to ad limit restriction. Alternatively, running video content may lead to better performance as we’ve seen on Tiktok.

### **  
****Ad Level (Creative) Performance Analysis: Lead Generation Focus**

The top-performing ads are hyper-specific about the program level (diploma, bachelors) and field of study (businessadministration, HospitalityManagement). The bottom performers are either too broad (generic\_certificate) or target niche fields (logisticsandsupplychains, interiordesign) that may have a smaller, more expensive audience to reach.

**

**Context Note:** Google has recently limited search ads to RSA, and limited to 3 RSA per ad group. Based on that we were struggling to launch the remaining ads, however now we are pivoting and creating more niche ad groups to allow for more overall ads.

#### **Campaign Performance**

While PMax campaigns account for the majority of spend and volume, traditional Search campaigns are currently delivering conversions at a slightly more efficient CPL (RM 98.57 vs RM 104.82). **Competitor Search campaigns** are the most cost-effective source of leads, achieving CPLs below RM 35. Conversely, Search campaigns targeting niche, high-level programs like Accounting and MBA are the least efficient, with CPLs exceeding RM 300.  
  

### **Audience / Targeting Performance (Ad Group Level)**

  - Ad Group level performance data was not available in the provided export. A granular analysis of audience and targeting performance cannot be completed at this time.  
      

### **Creative Performance (Search Keyword Level)**

  - Keyword level performance data was not available in the provided export. A granular analysis of keyword performance cannot be completed at this time.  
      

### **Action Steps**

1.  **Recommended Action:** Increase the daily budget for the two Competitor Search campaigns.
2.  **Recommended Action:** Scale down on the accounting and mba Search campaigns.

•⁠  ⁠PMax campaigns were paused.

•⁠  ⁠⁠The keyword “postgraduate diploma in teaching” was added to the Postgraduate Diploma in Education campaign at the middle of last week. So far, it has generated 60 impressions and 3 clicks, but no conversions yet. As it is newly launched, performance will be monitored further.

**Questions on Traffic from AI platforms and attributing these traffic channels**

  

1.  Whether there are any indirect or workaround methods that can help us improve attribution of AI-assisted traffic?
      
    1.  Currently, there are no reliable alternatives because attribution fundamentally depends on whether AI platforms pass through UTM-tagged URLs or identifiable referrer data
    2.  GA4 should be the most accurate and valid benchmark since we can already see data coming in, and the quality of the traffic from AI platforms
    3.  One way we can go about this is by creating a dedicated Default Channel Group in GA4 for AI Platforms (e.g. “AI Referrals”) covering sources such as ChatGPT, Gemini, Copilot, Perplexity, etc.
2.  Whether you recommend embedding UTM-tagged URLs in our public-facing assets (blogs, press releases, landing pages, TikTok descriptions, PDFs, partner pages etc.) so that if AI platforms surface those links, the UTMs will carry through to GA4.
      
    1.  Yes, we can embed UTMs into certain assets since AI tools don’t add UTMs themselves and they generally do not strip existing parameters.
    2.  However, its not 100% guaranteed but this is a good start to identify which content is being referenced by AI and how it assist in driving traffic.
    3.  One thing we should also note is we shouldn’t include UTMs on every asset since it might affect organic data, but focus on high-value content such as High-intent pages (programme pages, application pages), Campaign-driven contents, and press releases.
    4.  We should also check with SEO team on which content we should tag.
3.  Whether there are any technical setups (server-side tagging, CAPI, content structuring, or link standardisation) that you would suggest to help us better capture AI-driven clicks?
      
    1.  Few things we can conduct:
          
        1.  Grouping of AI traffic from unassigned channels into its own Default channel group in GA4
        2.  Implement consistent UTM structure across the high-value content
        3.  Server side tracking to improve attribution clarity and preserve the utm\_source.
4.  If you’ve seen other brands/clients successfully track AI referrals. How did they do it?
      
    1.  At present, we do not see any brands/clients that successfully track AI referrals because of the limitations on this platforms.
    2.  At most they are mainly conducting GA4 segmentation and behavioural analysis of the AI traffic.

**Insight:**

  - #### T2 remains our highest-converting campaign, driven by healthy CPMs and strong CVR. Targeting major cities across Peninsular Malaysia, this campaign continues to perform well, with BM creatives resonating particularly strongly with these audiences.
  - T1a is our second-best performer. Although its CPM is higher, its strong CTR helps keep CPL low. That said, CPM in this segment fluctuates easily, so we should avoid scaling the budget too aggressively.
  - The TVET Early Childhood Education creative delivered exceptional performance with 61 conversions — driven by a strong message, highly relevant BM copy, and an emotionally resonant problem–solution hook. Its appeal to Peninsular Malaysia audiences, combined with a clear value proposition (SKM certification, job pathway, and allowance), contributed to superior conversion efficiency.

  

#### 

#### **Action Steps**

1.  Increase budget slightly for T2  as they deliver the strongest conversions.
2.  Maintain T1a with controlled spend due to CPM fluctuations.
3.  Refresh BOF retargeting creatives with stronger enrollment-focused hooks to drive higher conversion intent.
4.  Looking into creating **content-rich lead forms**. These appear similar to simple landing pages, offering greater information to the user, increasing chance for higher intent users to convert.

| Aud\\\_Unitar\\\_MY\\\_SEM\\\_Brand\\\_T1a\\\_Max\\\_Conversion\\\_TCPA | RM8,788.82 | 73 | RM120 |
| Aud\\\_Unitar\\\_MY\\\_SEM\\\_Brand\\\_T2\\\_Max\\\_Conversion\\\_TCPA | RM6,562.53 | 73 | RM90 |

**Insight:** The Search campaigns segmented by Level of Study emerged as the top performance drivers this week, supported by a higher budget allocation, followed by the Brand T1a and Brand T2 campaigns. However, the Level of Study campaigns experienced a significant 33% increase in CPL week-on-week, driven by higher CPCs, likely due to the increased budget allocation.

**Action Steps**

1.  Reduce budgets during the upcoming long holiday period to maintain CPL efficiency.
2.  To decrease tCPA across campaigns  to allow higher spend and capturing more search volume.

**Insight:**

  - BOF retargeting improved, delivering 3 conversions, exceeding its usual baseline of 0–1 conversions, though CPL remains high due to limited audience size.
  - Scaling efforts successfully increased total conversions month-on-month, validating demand potential on TikTok.
  - T2 continues to show strong audience–message fit, while T1a remains effective but sensitive to CPM and CVR fluctuations.

  

#### **Action Steps**

1.  Prioritize T1a and T2 for controlled scaling, focusing on incremental budget increases rather than aggressive expansion.
2.  Optimize creative quality at MOF, especially with stronger enrollment and outcome-driven hooks, to recover CVR and improve CPL at scale.
3.  Maintain BOF retargeting with refreshed creatives, leveraging proven messaging to further improve lower-funnel efficiency.

**Insight:**

  - T2 remained the top-performing MOF campaign, delivering the highest conversion volume (63) with improved efficiency. CPC and CPM trended downward week-on-week, contributing to a significantly lower CPL (RM109.80 vs RM173.84 last period).
  - BOF retargeting remained limited in scale this week, with low conversion volume due to a smaller retargeting pool, though engagement metrics remained stable.
  - Awareness campaigns delivered consistent reach at low CPM, supporting upper-funnel replenishment ahead of the upcoming festive period.

  
  

#### **Action Steps**

1.  Prioritize T2 for controlled scaling, given its strong balance of volume and efficiency.
2.  Maintain T1a spend while refreshing conversion-driven creatives to improve CVR before increasing budget.
3.  Keep T1b active as an efficiency-focused campaign, leveraging its low CPL.
4.  Refresh BOF creatives with stronger enrollment hooks to better capitalize on warm audiences.

Our total investment for December was **RM231,564**, which was a increase of 35% week on week. We generated **1,788  leads** at a blended Cost Per Lead (CPL) of **RM129.5**. **Lead volume decreased by 3% and CPL increased by 38%**, compared to the previous period. This was an alarming spike and action was taken immediately to resolve this. The spike was due to:  
  
\- Google CPL spiked up to RM600 on 20th-21st January due to a recaptcha issue on the website which was highlighted by Audaura. The issue was resolved by the 22nd. Google ads recovered CPL by the 22nd.  
  

\- To allow Google to scale and capture search volume, our TCPA is still relaxed, with the intent to tighten it further by EOW.  
  
\- CPL on Meta increased due to us pausing ads end of last week. Optimisations have been made to resolve this.

| \*\*Website Conversion\*\* | \\- | \\- | \\- |

**Insight:** Majority of the budget continues on Instant Forms, delivering 1,123 leads at RM75.5 CPL, with a small part of the budget assigned to message conversation focused campaigns.

**Insight:** The new structure campaigns are generating good lead volume at low CPL at RM36.6. However, week on week we saw a spike in overall CPL after pausing a significant amount of ads previous week. We now have the capacity to run 86 additional ads on Meta.

**

### **Overall Google Summary**

|  |  |  |  |
| :-: | :-: | :-: | :-: |

| Search | RM125,228.48 | 541 | RM231 |

### **  
****Insight:** Search campaign spend increased by 227% week-on-week after relaxing the target CPA across all campaigns, indicating that Google is gradually ramping up delivery. Conversions increased from 335 to 541 leads during the same period.

### However, CPL also rose significantly from RM112 to RM231. The spike was mainly observed on 20–21 Jan, driven by an \~80% drop in conversion rate across all campaigns. Therefore, campaigns were temporarily paused to investigate website issues and were reactivated once the issue was resolved.

**Insight:** The Search campaigns segmented by “Level of Study” became the top drivers of performance, followed by the “Post grad” and “Brand” campaigns. 

**Action Steps**

1.  Reduced the tCPA from RM200 to RM100 to decrease the CPL.

  - Updated Awareness creatives performed better, delivering higher CTR and efficient CPM, supporting stronger downstream performance.

  

#### **Action Steps**

1.  Continue running the fee waiver promotion across MOF, prioritizing T1a and T2 to maximize conversion uplift while monitoring CPL stability.
2.  Sustain Awareness creative rotation, keeping promotional messaging fresh to avoid fatigue and maintain top-of-funnel efficiency.
3.  Scale MOF budgets gradually, using CVR as the primary guardrail rather than CTR alone.

## **Google****  
  
**

### **Overall Google Summary**

|  |  |  |  |
| :-: | :-: | :-: | :-: |
| \*\*Campaign Type\*\* | \*\*Cost\*\* | \*\*Conversions\*\* | \*\*CPL\*\* |
| PMax | \\- | \\- | \\- |
| Search | RM273,559 | 1,799 | RM152.08 |

### **  
****Insight:** Compared to December, Search campaign spend decreased by 16% as the campaigns did not actively spend at the beginning of the month after being reactivated following the pause at the end of December. To encourage delivery, we relaxed the bidding by increasing the tCPA, which allowed the system to start spending again.

### However, the CPL increased significantly from RM116 to RM152. The spike was mainly observed on 20–21 January and was driven by an approximately 80% drop in conversion rate across all campaigns. As a result, the campaigns were temporarily paused to investigate potential website issues and were reactivated once the issue was resolved. In parallel, we reduced the tCPA and proactively excluded irrelevant search terms to help stabilise performance and reduce CPL.

**Top 3 Campaigns by Conversions:**

|  |  |  |  |
| :-: | :-: | :-: | :-: |
| \*\*Campaign\*\* | \*\*Cost (RM)\*\* | \*\*Conv.\*\* | \*\*CPL (RM)\*\* |
| Aud\\\_Unitar\\\_MY\\\_SEM\\\_Level of study\\\_Low intent\\\_T1a+T2\\\_EN+BM\\\_Max\\\_Conversion\\\_TCPA | RM28,504.36 | 196 | RM145 |
| Aud\\\_Unitar\\\_MY\\\_SEM\\\_Brand\\\_T2\\\_Max\\\_Conversion\\\_TCPA\\\_Test | RM13,476.83 | 134 | RM100 |
| Aud\\\_Unitar\\\_MY\\\_SEM\\\_postgrad\\\_T1a\\\_EN\\\_Max\\\_Conversion\\\_TCPA | RM21,596.26 | 121 | RM178 |

**Insight:** The Search campaigns segmented by Level of Study emerged as the top performance drivers, followed by the Postgraduate and Brand campaigns. In terms of cost efficiency, the Brand campaign delivered a strong CPL performance. As a result, we reallocated additional budget to this campaign, allowing it to achieve 0% Search Impression Share lost due to budget.

**Action Steps**

1.  Reduced the tCPA to RM70 to decrease the CPL.
2.  Recommended launching a Demand Gen campaign, as we now have a sufficient lead pool. This will allow us to build lookalike audiences and expand reach to high-intent, similar users efficiently.

## **TikTok**

|  |  |
| :-: | :-: |

### **Performance Analysis**

**Top 5 Campaigns by Conversions:**

|  |  |  |  |
| :-: | :-: | :-: | :-: |
| \*\*Campaign\*\* | \*\*Cost (RM)\*\* | \*\*Conv.\*\* | \*\*CPL (RM)\*\* |
| audaura\\\_tiktok\\\_MOF\\\_InstantForms\\\_ConversionLeads\\\_A1\\\_T1a\\\_CBO | RM30,819. | 244 | RM126 |
| audaura\\\_tiktok\\\_MOF\\\_InstantForms\\\_ConversionLeads\\\_A1\\\_T2\\\_CBO | RM31,021 | 232 | RM133 |
| audaura\\\_tiktok\\\_MOF\\\_InstantForms\\\_ConversionLeads\\\_A1\\\_T1b\\\_CBO | RM​​22,401 | 164 | RM136 |
| audaura\\\_tiktok\\\_BOF\\\_InstantForms\\\_ConversionLeads\\\_A3\\\_T1aT1bT2\\\_CBO | RM6,171 | 4 | RM404 |

  

**Insight:**

  - Overall performance improved in January, with higher conversion volume and stronger efficiency across MOF campaigns compared to December.
  - The post-holiday period also contributed positively, as user intent normalized after the festive season, supporting stronger conversion performance compared to December.
  - The introduction of the new creatives played a key role in lifting performance, resulting in:
      
      - Lower CPC and CPM across all MOF campaigns
      - Meaningful conversion growth, most notably:
          
          - T1a: +23.86% conversions
          - T2: +24.73% conversions
          - T1b: +60.78% conversions
  - CPL improved month-on-month across MOF, indicating better conversion efficiency as scale increased, rather than diminishing returns.
  - BOF retargeting showed incremental improvement in volume, though CPL remains higher due to limited audience size.
  - Awareness campaigns delivered stronger reach and engagement, with higher impressions and lower CPM, supporting funnel replenishment into MOF due to the refresh of creatives.

#### **Action Steps**

1.  Continue prioritizing MOF campaigns, especially T1a and T2, as the core growth drivers.
2.  Maintain fee waiver messaging, while gradually testing urgency-based variations to sustain momentum.
3.  Scale budgets incrementally, using CPL as the primary guardrail to avoid efficiency erosion.
4.  Keep BOF spend controlled, focusing on creative refinement rather than aggressive scaling.

## **Google****  
  
**

### **Overall Google Summary**

|  |  |  |  |
| :-: | :-: | :-: | :-: |
| \*\*Campaign Type\*\* | \*\*Cost\*\* | \*\*Conversions\*\* | \*\*CPL\*\* |
| PMax | \\- | \\- | \\- |
| Search | RM41,491.69 | 362 | RM114.6 |

### **  
****Insight:** Compared to the previous week, Search campaign spend decreased by 56%, which led to conversions dropping from 811 to 362. The reduction in spend was driven by lowering the tCPA to RM80 in order to improve efficiency. As a result, the CPL decreased slightly from RM118 to RM113.  

### Additionally, search volume declined by 38% week-on-week. Despite the lower spend, Search Impression Share (SIS) lost by budget reduction of 87%, achieved at 1%, indicating that budget limitation was no longer a key constraint. The decline in performance was therefore mainly influenced by reduced demand and stricter bidding strategy rather than budget insufficiency.

**Top 3 Campaigns by Conversions:**

|  |  |  |  |
| :-: | :-: | :-: | :-: |
| \*\*Campaign\*\* | \*\*Cost (RM)\*\* | \*\*Conv.\*\* | \*\*CPL (RM)\*\* |
| Aud\\\_Unitar\\\_MY\\\_SEM\\\_Brand\\\_T1a\\\_Max\\\_Conversion\\\_TCPA\\\_Test | RM3,717.22 | 35 | RM105 |
| Aud\\\_Unitar\\\_MY\\\_SEM\\\_SPM\\\_T1b\\\_BM\\\_Max\\\_Conversion\\\_TCPA | RM2,715.93 | 30 | RM91 |
| Aud\\\_Unitar\\\_MY\\\_SEM\\\_SPM\\\_T1a\\\_BM\\\_Max\\\_Conversion\\\_TCPA | RM2,412.81 | 28 | RM86 |

**Insight:** The Search campaigns segmented by Level of Study emerged as the top performance drivers, followed by the Postgraduate and Brand campaigns. In terms of cost efficiency, the Brand campaign delivered a strong CPL performance. As a result, we reallocated additional budget to this campaign, allowing it to achieve 0% Search Impression Share lost due to budget.

SPM campaigns in BM are driving good performance as well.

**Action Steps**

1.  Increase the tCPA to RM100 to see scale up potential.
2.  Reduce the budget during this upcoming long holiday.
3.  Recommended launching a Demand Gen campaign, as we now have a sufficient lead pool. This will allow us to build lookalike audiences and expand reach to high-intent, similar users efficiently.
4.  CMS for Google Ads server side tracking with Cloudflare.
      
    1.  Cloudflare Content Delivery Network - 5 to 10 mins tracking
    2.  Benefits:
          
        1.  Performance Uplift - Get more data to identify which campaigns are driving conversions and also relay the info back to the campaigns.
        2.  Privacy by default - Protect user’s privacy while we can still gather data for campaign learnings
        3.  Easy setup
    3.  Access:
          
        1.  Google Ads Admin access
        2.  Cloudflare credential: Email and password
        3.  The person will need both Google Ads and Cloudflare access

### **Overall TikTok Summary**

|  |  |
| :-: | :-: |
| \*\*Metric\*\* | \*\*Value\*\* |

| \*\*Conversions\*\* | 132 |
| \*\*CPL\*\* | RM163 |

  

### **Performance Analysis**

**Top 5 Campaigns by Conversions:**

|  |  |  |  |
| :-: | :-: | :-: | :-: |
| \*\*Campaign\*\* | \*\*Cost (RM)\*\* | \*\*Conv.\*\* | \*\*CPL (RM)\*\* |
| audaura\\\_tiktok\\\_MOF\\\_InstantForms\\\_ConversionLeads\\\_A1\\\_T1a\\\_CBO | RM30,819. | 244 | RM126 |
| audaura\\\_tiktok\\\_MOF\\\_InstantForms\\\_ConversionLeads\\\_A1\\\_T2\\\_CBO | RM31,021 | 232 | RM133 |
| audaura\\\_tiktok\\\_MOF\\\_InstantForms\\\_ConversionLeads\\\_A1\\\_T1b\\\_CBO | RM​​22,401 | 164 | RM136 |
| audaura\\\_tiktok\\\_BOF\\\_InstantForms\\\_ConversionLeads\\\_A3\\\_T1aT1bT2\\\_CBO | RM6,171 | 4 | RM404 |

  

**Insight:**

  - Overall traffic efficiency improved following the creative refresh, with CTR increasing across MOF campaigns (T1a, T2, T1b) — indicating stronger hook and message-market fit.
  - CPC remained stable to slightly improved in most campaigns, suggesting the new creatives are generating clicks more efficiently.
  - The CPL increase was primarily driven by softer CVR, not traffic inefficiency — indicating that while more users are clicking, a smaller proportion are completing the lead form.
  - The addition of the fee waiver promotion likely increased curiosity-driven clicks, broadening audience engagement but slightly diluting conversion intent.
  - BOF performance remained stable, with improved CTR and maintained engagement, suggesting warm audiences are still responsive.
  - The recent creative refresh may have triggered a short learning phase reset, contributing to temporary volatility in conversion efficiency.

  

#### **Action Steps**

1.  Maintain controlled BOF budget increase to continue capturing incremental warm conversions without oversaturating the audience.
2.  Allow refreshed creatives to stabilize (5–7 days) before making major structural changes.
3.  Continue strengthening TOF to expand retargeting pools and sustain BOF momentum.
4.  To set up MBA12K dedicated campaigns

| \*\*Spend\*\* | RM26,133.59 |
| \*\*Conversions\*\* | 94 |
| \*\*CPL\*\* | RM278 |

  

### **Performance Analysis**

**Top 5 Campaigns by Conversions:**

|  |  |  |  |
| :-: | :-: | :-: | :-: |
| \*\*Campaign\*\* | \*\*Cost (RM)\*\* | \*\*Conv.\*\* | \*\*CPL (RM)\*\* |
| audaura\\\_tiktok\\\_MOF\\\_InstantForms\\\_ConversionLeads\\\_A1\\\_T1a\\\_CBO | RM7,089.53 | 27 | RM262 |
| audaura\\\_tiktok\\\_MOF\\\_InstantForms\\\_ConversionLeads\\\_A1\\\_T2\\\_CBO | RM7,368.15 | 27 | RM272 |
| audaura\\\_tiktok\\\_MOF\\\_InstantForms\\\_ConversionLeads\\\_A1\\\_Masters\\\_CBO | RM2,900 | 20 | RM145 |
| audaura\\\_tiktok\\\_MOF\\\_InstantForms\\\_ConversionLeads\\\_A1\\\_T1b\\\_CBO | RM​​5,554.42  | 19 | RM292 |
| audaura\\\_tiktok\\\_BOF\\\_InstantForms\\\_ConversionLeads\\\_A3\\\_T1aT1bT2\\\_CBO | RM419 | 1 | RM419 |

  

**Insight:**

  - T1a performance softened, with conversions declining  and CPL increasing, despite stronger CTR. This suggests improved engagement but weaker downstream conversion efficiency.
  - MBA campaign launched with positive early signals, generating 20 conversions at a competitive CPL (145 MYR), indicating strong initial audience-product fit.
  - The increase in CPL was primarily driven by softer conversion intent and higher auction competition during the Chinese New Year period, as campaigns delivered through the festive season, impacting CVR despite stable engagement metrics.

#### **Action Steps**

1.  Lower spend on T1a and reallocate budget toward stronger-performing segments.
2.  Gradually increase budget for the Masters campaign (10–15%) to validate scalability while maintaining efficiency. If the performance is stable by mid-week, We’ll gradually add more creative.
3.  Maintain controlled spend on T2, optimizing toward CVR improvement.

| audaura\\\_tiktok\\\_MOF\\\_InstantForms\\\_ConversionLeads\\\_A1\\\_Masters\\\_CBO | RM4,109.78   | 23 | RM178 |
| audaura\\\_tiktok\\\_MOF\\\_InstantForms\\\_ConversionLeads\\\_A1\\\_T1a\\\_CBO | RM5,607.74 | 20 | RM280 |
| audaura\\\_tiktok\\\_MOF\\\_InstantForms\\\_ConversionLeads\\\_A1\\\_T2\\\_CBO | RM5,929.45 | 20 | RM296 |
| audaura\\\_tiktok\\\_MOF\\\_InstantForms\\\_ConversionLeads\\\_A1\\\_T1b\\\_CBO | RM​​5,616.07 | 15 | RM374 |

**Insight:**

  - Performance is starting to recover, with improvements seen in conversion volume across selected segments, indicating early signs of stabilization.
  - Masters campaign showed strong pickup this week, delivering improved conversions and better efficiency, reinforcing it as a key growth driver.
  - CTR remains healthy across campaigns, suggesting creatives and messaging continue to resonate well with the audience.
  - However, CPL remains elevated, mainly due to higher CPM and lingering CVR softness following the recent lead form changes. \[\*lead form changed on 2nd March)
  - T1a and T2 continue to face efficiency pressure, as higher costs and lower conversion rates from previous weeks are still impacting blended performance.

  

#### **Action Steps**

1.  Scale Masters campaign by +15–20% budget as it shows strongest recovery and lowest CPL trend.
2.  Reduce T1a budget by -10–15% to control CPL, as efficiency has not fully recovered.
3.  Maintain T2 at current spend for another 3–5 days to validate CVR stabilization before scaling or reducing.
4.  Reduce T1b slightly by -10% due to weaker efficiency and declining conversion volume.
5.  Keep BOF at minimal spend to maintain retargeting coverage without overspending on a limited pool.
6.  Set a short-term CPL target reduction of \~10–15% over the next 7 days, driven by CVR recovery post lead form change.
7.  Monitor CVR daily (target: +10–20% improvement) — if no recovery within 5–7 days, revisit lead form structure to reduce friction.

1.  Keep BOF at minimal spend
2.  Monitor lead form performance daily

→ Pause lowest-performing variant once statistically clear

### **  
****Insight:** This month, campaign performance has shown a strong recovery. Compared to the previous month, Search campaign spend increased by 170%, while CPL improved significantly, decreasing from RM128.54 to RM92.

### As a result, total leads increased substantially by 279%, growing from 665 leads to 2,526 leads.

  

### **Top 3 Campaigns by Conversions:**

|  |  |  |  |
| :-: | :-: | :-: | :-: |
| \*\*Campaign\*\* | \*\*Cost (RM)\*\* | \*\*Conv.\*\* | \*\*CPL (RM)\*\* |
| Aud\\\_Unitar\\\_MY\\\_SEM\\\_Level of study\\\_Low intent\\\_T1a+T2\\\_EN+BM\\\_Max\\\_Conversion\\\_TCPA | RM34,225.28 | 392 | RM87.25 |
| Aud\\\_Unitar\\\_MY\\\_SEM\\\_Brand\\\_T1a\\\_Max\\\_Conversion\\\_TCPA | RM34,930.73 | 245 | RM142.62 |
| Aud\\\_Unitar\\\_MY\\\_SEM\\\_Brand\\\_T2\\\_Max\\\_Conversion\\\_TCPA | RM34,930.73 | 222 | RM111.70 |

**Insight:** The Level of study segmented campaigns became the top performance driver this month, followed by Brand campaigns.

**Additional Observation & Strategic Insight**

However, a month-on-month increase in CPC was observed for Brand campaigns, particularly for Brand\_T1a\_Max\_Conversion\_TCPA and Brand\_T2\_Max\_Conversion\_TCPA, where CPC increased from RM2 to RM4 and RM1.6 to RM3.7, respectively.

Despite this, Auction Insights indicate that competitor activity remains relatively low, with no significant increase in impression share or position dominance.

This suggests that the rise in CPC is likely driven by internal factors, such as:

  - Budget scaling, leading to participation in more auctions at higher bid thresholds

**Action Steps & Ongoing Optimisation**

  - Continue to monitor CPC trends and Auction Insights for any changes in competitor behaviour
  - Review search term and auction overlap to minimise internal competition
  - Optimise bid strategy and budget allocation to maintain efficiency while sustaining lead volume
  - Launched an A/B test for AI Max in Brand campaigns, with performance monitored over a one-month period.
      
      - Based on initial results, the AI Max campaign generated 44 leads with a CVR of 4.7% and CPL of RM76.54. In comparison, the original campaign, despite lower spend, delivered 17 leads with a more efficient CPL of RM54 and higher CVR of 5.86%.
      - Given the lower efficiency, the AI Max campaign will be discontinued, with budget reallocated to higher-performing campaigns.
  - Launched new target location campaigns, which will be closely monitored and optimised based on performance.
  - It is recommended to refresh ad copy to prevent creative fatigue and improve engagement, including testing new messaging angles such as value propositions, urgency, and location-based benefits.

|  |  |  |  |

| \*\*Campaign\*\* | \*\*Cost (RM)\*\* | \*\*Conv.\*\* | \*\*CPL (RM)\*\* |
| audaura\\\_tiktok\\\_MOF\\\_InstantForms\\\_ConversionLeads\\\_A1\\\_Masters\\\_CBO | RM4,109.78   | 23 | RM178 |
| audaura\\\_tiktok\\\_MOF\\\_InstantForms\\\_ConversionLeads\\\_A1\\\_T1a\\\_CBO | RM5,607.74 | 20 | RM280 |
| audaura\\\_tiktok\\\_MOF\\\_InstantForms\\\_ConversionLeads\\\_A1\\\_T2\\\_CBO | RM5,929.45 | 20 | RM296 |
| audaura\\\_tiktok\\\_MOF\\\_InstantForms\\\_ConversionLeads\\\_A1\\\_T1b\\\_CBO | RM​​5,616.07 | 15 | RM374 |
| audaura\\\_tiktok\\\_BOF\\\_InstantForms\\\_ConversionLeads\\\_A3\\\_T1aT1bT2\\\_CBO | RM699.92 | 0 |   |

  

**Insight:**

  - March saw a +28% increase in spend and +110% growth in traffic (clicks), indicating strong audience engagement. However, conversions declined (-31%), resulting in higher CPL.
  - CPM stayed elevated throughout the month, reflecting sustained auction competition post-CNY and into Raya, adding cost pressure and reducing traffic quality.
  - At the same time, Ramadan period introduced a behavioral shift among the core target audience, where users are more likely to browse but delay decision-making, resulting in lower CVR despite consistent engagement.
  - The lead form update on 2nd March may have contributed to a slight increase in conversion friction, particularly as it was introduced during a softer demand period, which likely impacted CVR and resulted in higher CPL.
  - the Masters campaign showed recovery signals, delivering higher conversion volume in March (57 conversions vs 36 in February, +58%), indicating that underlying demand remains strong when value proposition resonates with the audience.
  - End-of-month lead form optimisation marks a key intervention point, expected to support CVR recovery and improve funnel efficiency heading into April

#### **Action Steps**

1.  Reduce T1a budget by -10–15% to control CPL as efficiency continues to decline
2.  Increase Masters campaign budget by +15–20% to leverage strongest recovery signal
3.  Maintain T2 at current spend for 3–5 days to validate stability before scaling or reducing
4.  Keep BOF at minimal spend to maintain retargeting coverage without oversaturating audience
5.  Monitor lead form CVR daily over the next 5–7 days as key recovery indicator
6.  If CVR does not improve, iterate form structure (reduce fields / simplify flow to lower friction)

**Top 5 Campaigns by Conversions:**

|  |  |  |  |
| :-: | :-: | :-: | :-: |
| \*\*Campaign\*\* | \*\*Cost (RM)\*\* | \*\*Conv.\*\* | \*\*CPL (RM)\*\* |
| audaura\\\_tiktok\\\_MOF\\\_InstantForms\\\_ConversionLeads\\\_A1\\\_T2\\\_CBO | RM6,402.69   | 130 | RM49.25 |
| audaura\\\_tiktok\\\_MOF\\\_InstantForms\\\_ConversionLeads\\\_A1\\\_T1a\\\_CBO | RM4,694.41 | 101 | RM46.48 |
| audaura\\\_tiktok\\\_MOF\\\_InstantForms\\\_ConversionLeads\\\_A1\\\_Masters\\\_CBO | RM5,229.75 | 78 | RM67.05 |
| audaura\\\_tiktok\\\_MOF\\\_InstantForms\\\_ConversionLeads\\\_A1\\\_T1b\\\_CBO | RM​​5,041.02  | 72 | RM70.01 |
| audaura\\\_tiktok\\\_BOF\\\_InstantForms\\\_ConversionLeads\\\_A3\\\_T1aT1bT2\\\_CBO | RM699.79 | 1 | RM699.79 |

  

**Insight:**

  - Performance saw a strong recovery this week, with conversion volume increasing across all MOF campaigns, indicating CVR has rebounded following recent lead form optimisation.
  - The improvement aligns with both lead form enhancements taking effect and the SPM announcement period, which has driven higher urgency and intent among prospective students.
  - CPL declined significantly across campaigns (e.g. T2: \~474 → 49 MYR, T1b: \~624 → 70 MYR), suggesting prior inefficiencies were primarily driven by conversion friction rather than traffic quality.
  - Traffic quality remains stable (CTR relatively unchanged), reinforcing that performance recovery is driven by improved conversion efficiency rather than changes in traffic.
  - All major segments (T1a, T1b, T2, Masters) are contributing to the uplift, with T2 and T1a leading in conversion volume recovery, indicating a broad-based return in demand.

#### **Action Steps**

1.  Gradually scale T2 and T1a by +10–20% to capitalise on strong volume recovery while maintaining CPL control
2.  Increase Masters budget by +10–15%, with close monitoring on CPL stability
3.  Maintain T1b at current spend until conversion trend stabilises further
4.  Reintroduce BOF with controlled budget allocation (\~5–10%) to capture rising retargeting demand
5.  Monitor CPL daily, targeting stabilisation within \~60–80 MYR blended over the next 3–5 days
6.  If performance sustains for 3 consecutive days, unlock an additional +10% budget scaling across MOF campaigns***

**Insight:** TikTok was the standout channel this week, with MOF Instant Forms efficiency improving from RM89.78 → RM79.81 CPL (-11%) and lead volume going up by 11% (234 → 261) on slightly lower spend. Within MOF, **T1a took over as a winning campaign at RM60.26 CPL / 84 leads**, the best CPL we’ve seen on TikTok this cycle (improved from RM71.86 last week). The A1\_Masters audience recovered sharply from RM131.46 → RM83.71 (-36%), **reversing last week’s read** that postgrad intent was structurally inefficient on the platform. The T2 campaign increased modestly (RM75.84 → RM82.47) but remains within target. **T1b held flat at RM106.89 across two consecutive weeks**, confirming a creative refresh is required to drive CPL down. BOF Retargeting delivered **zero leads on RM700 spend** this week (vs 3 leads last week), reinforcing that the retargeting pool is too small / saturated to drive efficient conversion at current spend. TOF Awareness was dialled back \~20% (RM4,601 → RM3,700) in line with month-end pacing.

**Action Steps**

  - **Scale T1a MOF Instant Forms**: now the best CPL on the account at RM60.26, with clear headroom to absorb incremental spend within the 20%.
  - **Refresh creative on T1b MOF Instant Forms**: CPL has held at RM106.89 for two consecutive weeks, \~33% above the next-worst tier (T2). Test new hooks, swap top-of-feed creative, and tighten audience signals before considering spend reduction.
  - **Hold A1\_Masters MOF Instant Forms allocation**: sharp recovery from RM131 → RM83.71 warrants one more week of reading before any reallocation decision. Reversing last week’s call to deprioritise; we now have evidence the audience can perform on TikTok with the right creative cycle.
  - **Pause and restructure BOF Retargeting**: zero leads this week vs 3 last week confirms the retargeting pool is too small / saturated to drive efficient conversion. Pause current placements and rebuild the audience via retargeting website visitors, social viewers, video watchers (move off interest-based only)

Our total investment for April was **RM890,369.53**, an increase of **\~28%** month on month as we scaled into the SPM results window.  
We generated **14,958 leads** at a blended Cost Per Lead (CPL) of **RM59.52**. **Lead volume increased by \~165%** while **CPL improved by \~52%** compared to March  
This was likely driven by:  
\-  SPM results release   
\- C2 2026 Meta structure Launch  
\- Google scaling Search spend by 127% to capture the SPM demand wave  
\- Late-March TikTok lead form optimisation removing the CVR friction that softened March performance.   
  
Every channel improved CPL materially MoM: Meta **62%**, Google **37%**, TikTok **63%.**

### **  
  
**

### **Insight:** Google Search performance scaled dramatically in April: spend **+127% MoM**, leads +**263%**, and CPL **-37%** driving the strongest single-channel efficiency uplift on the account this year.

### Two possible factors drove this.  
(1) SPM results window: peak intake intent pushed CVR sharply higher across all SEM segments, particularly Brand and Level of Study terms.  
(2) Keyword hygiene compounding: continued weekly cleanup on Level of Study\_Low Intent translated into a **40%** MoM CPL improvement on that campaign on **43%** higher spend.  
The dedicated SPM\_T2\_BM campaign also stood out at **RM42.87 CPL / 597 leads** driving one of the channel’s most efficient CPL of the month, validating the standalone SPM campaign as a strategy for future campaigns.

  

### **Top 3 Campaigns by Conversions:**

|  |  |  |  |
| :-: | :-: | :-: | :-: |
| \*\*Campaign\*\* | \*\*Cost (RM)\*\* | \*\*Conv.\*\* | \*\*CPL (RM)\*\* |
| Aud\\\_Unitar\\\_MY\\\_SEM\\\_Level of study\\\_Low intent\\\_T1a+T2\\\_EN+BM\\\_Max\\\_Conversion\\\_TCPA | RM49,041.07 | 940 | RM52.18 |
| Aud\\\_Unitar\\\_MY\\\_SEM\\\_SPM\\\_T2\\\_BM\\\_Max\\\_Conversion\\\_TCPA | RM25,583.87 | 597 | RM42.87 |
| Aud\\\_Unitar\\\_MY\\\_SEM\\\_Brand\\\_T2\\\_Max\\\_Conversion\\\_TCPA\\\_Test | RM30,292.82 | 595 | RM50.93 |

**Insight:** **Level of Study\_Low Intent** drove the highest volume**,** with the keyword hygiene work compounding into strong CPL improvements over time.  
Brand\_T2\_ Max Test outperformed the standard Brand\_T1a and Brand\_T2 campaign from the top 3 (**595 leads at RM50.93 CPL**), showing meaningful efficiency improvement over the March Brand baseline of **RM112–143.** Notably, Brand campaigns are no longer the dominant volume driver they were in earlier cycle months with Level of Study and SPM-specific terms now lead on both volume and efficiency.

**Action Taken:**

  - **Continued weekly keyword hygiene on Level of Study\_Low Intent across April:** pruned low-converting terms each week to compound the CPL improvement (RM87.25 → RM52.18 MoM).
  - **Launched a dedicated SPM\_T2\_BM campaign aligned to the SPM results release window:** delivered the channel’s best CPL at RM42.87 / 597 leads.
  - **Scaled Search spend +127% MoM to capture peak SPM-window demand without sacrificing efficiency:** leads scaled at 2x the rate of spend (+263% leads vs +127% spend).

 **Action Steps**

  - **Templatise the SPM campaign playbook for future result-release cycles (SPM Trial, STPM, A-Levels, IB):**  the dedicated tactical campaign delivered the channel’s best monthly CPL and should be a recurring pattern aligned to result-release calendars.
  - **Continuing weekly keyword hygiene cadence on Level of Study\_Low Intent**: this remains the most reliable efficiency lever on the channel and the only campaign that has compounded efficiency consistently month over month.

**Insight:** TikTok delivered a strong month-on-month uplift, with channel CPL improving from R**M346 → RM126.30 (-63%)** on roughly marginal spend increase **(RM131,527 → RM151,437, +15%)** and **3.2x** lead volume **(380 → 1,199)**. The MOF Instant Form campaigns were the main level of this improvement, with leads scaling from **351 → 1,181 (+236%)** and CPL improving from **RM272 → RM76 (-72%).** Three drivers stand out. (1) All four MOF tiers scaled efficiently: T2 (RM65.07), T1a (RM61.47), T1b (RM97.05), and A1\_Masters (RM98.51) all 4–5x’d both spend and conversions, with the March CPL ranges of RM178–374 improving to RM61–99. (2) A1\_Masters recovered: postgrad CPL on TikTok dropped from RM178 in March (lowest spend, best CPL of the month) to RM98.51 in April (5x spend at sub-RM100 CPL)  confirming postgrad demand activates meaningfully on the platform. (3) TOF Awareness scaled aggressively **(RM32,721 → RM59,043, +80%)** to expand the addressable audience pool feeding into MOF. 

  

**Creative split in terms of performance:****  
**- After analysis, the performance comparison between TOF and MOF funnel ads (in instant form campaigns) is very similar. We have MOF GIF ads running that have been performing for 2 cycle at the top, with similar performance to some TOF vlog style ads also in the mix in terms of CPL.

**MBA Video Performance:****  
**

**Action Steps**

  - **Sustain MOF spends through May to April pacing while monitoring CPL closely**:  post-SPM normalisation will likely lift CPL, so we should expect some efficiency reversion and budget appropriately. Maintain the four-tier MOF structure (T2, T1a, T1b, A1\_Masters) which has proven scalable.
  - **Rebuild the BOF retargeting audience**: the current pool (5 leads / RM2,633 / RM526.52 CPL) is not performing. Expand via MOF reach growth, video-view custom audiences, and engagement-based audiences before scaling BOF spend. 
  - **Quantify TOF Awareness contribution**: with TOF representing **\~39%** of TikTok spend in April **(RM59,043)**, run an assisted-conversion / view-through analysis to validate the funnel-building thesis and confirm the TOF investment is generating downstream MOF lift.
  - **Refresh creative on T1b**: at RM97.05 CPL compared to the other Leads Campaign. Test new hooks and tighten audience signals to close the gap with T1a (RM61.47) and T2 (RM65.07).

The new **SPM\_T1a\_EN** campaign at \#3 (46 leads / RM78.40 CPL) is a strong first-week read for a new tactical SPM variant running in parallel with the SPM\_T2\_BM template. 

**Action Taken:**

  - Reduced Search spend \~17% vs the 20–26 April benchmark to manage the post-SPM efficiency drag and protect the monthly budget envelope.

We’ll have to run a search-term audit for new high-cost queries, check Auction Insights for competitor bidding pressure, and review bid strategy and target CPA settings. 
2.  **Refresh ad copy across Brand and Level of Study** — moving fully into the post-SPM cycle, test new messaging angles (course duration, May intake urgency, scholarships, location-based benefits) to lift CTR and Quality Score and recover Brand efficiency.
3.  **Validate SPM\_T1a\_EN over 2 more weeks** — if it sustains sub-RM80 CPL, formalise it as a second SPM playbook variant alongside SPM\_T2\_BM for future result-release cycles (SPM Trial, STPM, etc.).

****Insight:** Google Search delivered **RM347,816 / 3,917 leads / RM88.79 CPL**, an increase  from April's RM57.38 (**+55%)** but still below the March CPL baseline of \~RM91.

### Spend pulled back **-34%** as the SPM intake window closed; leads -57%, with the lead drop outpacing spend by \~2:1 (post-SPM CVR normalisation, exactly the pattern flagged in April's Action Steps).

  

### **Top 3 Campaigns by Conversions:**

|  |  |  |  |
| :-: | :-: | :-: | :-: |
| \*\*Campaign\*\* | \*\*Cost (RM)\*\* | \*\*Conv.\*\* | \*\*CPL (RM)\*\* |
| Aud\\\_Unitar\\\_MY\\\_SEM\\\_Level of study\\\_Low intent\\\_T1a+T2\\\_EN+BM\\\_Max\\\_Conversion\\\_TCPA | RM27,363.45 | 365 | RM75.04 |
| Aud\\\_Unitar\\\_MY\\\_SEM\\\_Brand\\\_T1a\\\_Max\\\_Conversion\\\_TCPA | RM19,767.91 | 262 | RM75.40 |
| Aud\\\_Unitar\\\_MY\\\_SEM\\\_Brand\\\_T2\\\_Max\\\_Conversion\\\_TCPA\\\_Test | RM19,763.26 | 253 | RM78.03 |

**Insight:** Level of Study\_Low Intent stayed at \#1 but decayed in performance: **365 leads / RM75.04 CPL** vs April's 940 / RM52.18  showing that  volume -61%, CPL +44%. The weekly keyword hygiene work is still holding the line below the March baseline.

Brand\_T1a moved into \#2 at **262 leads / RM75.40 CPL**, not in April's top 3 (SPM\_T2\_BM and Brand\_T2 Test held those spots). The shift reflects the post-SPM topic mix moving back toward Brand as the dominant volume driver.

Both Brand campaigns have stabilised at \~RM75–78 which is  well above April's RM50.93 baseline but well below the March RM112–143 range. Brand has found a new mid-cycle equilibrium.

SPM\_T2\_BM (April's \#2 at 597 leads / RM42.87 CPL) is absent which is expected, given the SPM result window is closed. The dedicated tactical campaign is paused or scaled down, freeing budget for the post-SPM mix.

  

**Action Taken:**

  - **Pulled SEM spent back -34% MoM as SPM intake demand normalised:** held CPL drift to +55% rather than letting it return fully to March levels.
  - **Continued weekly keyword hygiene cadence on Level of Study\_Low Intent throughout May:** the campaign held below March efficiency even under post-SPM CVR pressure.

 **Action Steps**

  - **Consolidate the two duplicate Brand\_T2 campaigns into one:** running identical campaigns splits TCPA learning and risks self-competition on the same brand keywords. Keep the one with the stronger conversion history; pause the other.
  - **Templatise the SPM\_T2\_BM playbook for upcoming result-release cycles (STPM in June, A-Levels, IB):** April's RM42.87 CPL / 597 leads is the channel's best monthly outlier and should be a recurring tactical pattern aligned to result-release calendars.
  - **Push Brand toward the April baseline of RM50.93:** both Brand lines are now at \~RM75. A search-term audit, Auction Insights review and bid recalibration should close the gap.
  - **Maintain weekly keyword hygiene cadence on Level of Study\_Low Intent:** it remains the most reliable efficiency lever on the channel and the only campaign that has compounded efficiency consistently MoM.
  - **Set a realistic June baseline:** expect mild further drift before re-acceleration into the next result-release cycle; budget around a RM85–95 blended Google CPL rather than chasing the April level.
  - Begin set up for **Demand Gen** campaign focused on UC campuses.

**Performance  Questions**

**1.⁠ ⁠Based on current digital activity, are you seeing CPL trending upward for PG and UG programmes?**

|  |  |  |  |
| :-: | :-: | :-: | :-: |
| \*\*PG\*\* |  |  |  |
| \*\*Monthly\*\* | \*\*Cost\*\* | \*\*Conversions\*\* | \*\*CPL\*\* |

| \*\*Feb\*\* | RM47,701 | 405 | RM117.80 |
| \*\*Mar\*\* | RM48,707 | 516 | RM94.40 |
| \*\*Apr\*\* | RM83,268 | 1037 | RM80.29 |
| \*\*May\*\* | RM47,057 | 546 | RM86.23 |
| \*\*UG\*\* |  |  |  |
| \*\*Monthly\*\* | \*\*Cost\*\* | \*\*Conversions\*\* | \*\*CPL\*\* |
| \*\*Jan\*\* | RM313,659 | 2912 | RM107.71 |
| \*\*Feb\*\* | RM174,168 | 1410 | RM123.50 |
| \*\*Mar\*\* | RM264,195 | 2507 | RM105.33 |
| \*\*Apr\*\* | RM375,672 | 6551 | RM57.35 |
| \*\*May\*\* | RM260,937 | 3147 | RM82.64 |

  

PG CPL has fallen from RM101.23 in January to RM86.23 in May (-15%), and UG has come down from RM107.71 to RM82.64 (-23%).

The quarter-to-date view makes the trend clearer: PG Q1 average RM104.48 vs Q2-to-date RM83.26 (-20%); UG Q1 average RM112.18 vs Q2-to-date RM70.00 (-38%).

April sto

od out in terms of efficiency for both programmes which we’re assuming  is driven in part by the post-SPM intake surge on the UG side, which pulled CPL to RM57.35, and May has ticked up modestly off that low (UG +44% MoM, PG +7%). Both programmes remain comfortably below their Jan / Feb levels, and the directional read over the long haul is clearly down as we continue to diversify content and offerings on the platform.

**2.⁠ ⁠Which programme categories are showing the these increases?**

  

**3.⁠ ⁠Do we know if our competitors are spending more?**

Our competitor is definitely spending more.  
We can observe this based on the volume of traffic they’ve generated over the course of Feb - April (Data limitation).  
UM is driving the bulk of the traffic with APU taking second in total monthly visits.  
UM does have the upper hand in sheer volume as we observe the channel split in the images below:

In short, yes our competitors are spending more in Paid Social\* with UM & MSU leading in traffic generated over the course of the last few months.  
We do see other competitors taking a very different approach investing in display with Segi & APU leading the traffic volume, but have since tapered down since early April.  
**We are consistently investing heavier in paid search compared to our competitors** with UM & APU picking up their spend since late March.  
  
**So what does this mean?**  
Dominant competitors like UM are leading in most metrics because they have a much stronger branding play that’s been brewing for several years thus giving them the upper hand in other channels like Direct, Organic Search and Referrals.  
As we grow on our paid channels, we should look to also invest more into TOF campaigns that might not bear fruit in the short term but WILL benefit our long term branding exposure by adopting more Top of Funnel/Brand Awareness type marketing campaigns.

We do see the rise in GEO/ Gen AI search queries and it's something we have to tackle in the near future if not now. There are many ways to increase our digital footprint but expanding and strengthening our SEO efforts is a great start by generating more blogs/articles on the website. Another effective way to expand our GEO/Gen Ai exposure is through other content creators via blogs/articles/ or Youtube. AI is going to be the next Search soon and there is no harm in investing into creating an infrastructure that's ready to scale when the boom hits.

**5.⁠ ⁠We're seeing a trend in PG billboards - Are competitors primarily pushing PG in their paid campaigns too?****  
**No — not as a category-wide posture. We’re seeing that APU is running what seems to be a short, calendar-driven postgraduate surge timed to its 6–7 June 2026 Postgrad Open Day (Star StarPicks advertorial dated 3 June, LinkedIn "Move Up Your Career" Open Day push, MBA / DBA / MSc front-and-centre), For SEGi online-MBA price thread (RM21,712 — the kind of sharp number that lives well on a billboard).  
  
Beyond those two, the field is still UG-led: MSU's "PG campaign" landing page recycles the same generic copy as its foundation page (no working-adult VP, no PG-specific scholarship, no intake urgency — they're still in SPM-leaver / "Conditional Offer Letter Instantly" mode), and UM is structurally absent from paid PG altogether as a public research university, with zero traceable PG billboards.  
Net: APU's spike + SEGi's MBA thread is creating the visual impression of a trend, but two of the four named competitors are not in it — so matching with a sustained PG billboard push would be reading a campaign window as a category move.

UC Data:  
  
  
  
  
  
**Trends in the data****  
****  
**

  

1\. The two channels are strong in opposite states — your biggest arbitrage opportunity. Johor is the clearest case: Meta is broken there (RM314 CPL, only 2 leads) while Google is the account's volume leader (34 conversions at RM101). Melaka is the exact inverse — Meta's best big market (RM86.62) while Google struggles (RM147.31, only 3.7 conversions). Same pattern in Kelantan: Meta weak at RM152.66, Google cheapest in the whole account at RM66.40. Budget should not be split evenly per state — it should follow the winning channel per state.  
  

  

2\. East Malaysia carries the account, but only half of it efficiently. Kota Kinabalu is the best market overall — high volume on both channels and the cheapest blended CPL (RM86). Kuching gets the highest Meta spend in the account (RM3,562, 20% of Meta budget) but sits \~17% above blended average on both channels (RM118.72 Meta / RM115.74 Google). Kuching is buying volume, not efficiency — worth a creative/keyword refresh before adding more budget.

  

3\. Every Google campaign is budget-capped, and search top impression share is only \~22–27%. "Limited by budget" across the board means there's unclaimed cheap volume. The obvious scale candidates are the ones converting well below account CPL: Kelantan (RM66.40, only RM265 spent), Perak (RM84.77), and Kota Kinabalu (RM103 at 5.34% conv rate with 17.8K search volume). Kelantan especially — it's getting RM250/day budget but only spent RM266 in 14 days, suggesting the cap or bids are throttling it.

  

4\. Meta engagement quality looks strong in Sabah. KK generated 57 messaging conversations on 34 form leads — far above the account ratio (208 conversations / 181 leads). Those conversations are an extra intent signal worth tracking through to enrolment.

  

5\. Weakest blended markets: Penang, Pahang, Kelantan (RM117–124). Penang is below average on both channels (Meta RM104.97, Google RM131.60 at 4.02% conv rate) — neither channel has a clear edge there, so it's a creative/landing-page problem rather than a mix problem. Pahang's blend is dragged by Meta Kuantan (RM114) with almost no Google support.

  

Suggested moves: shift Meta budget Johor → Melaka/KK, shift Google budget Melaka → Kelantan/Perak/Johor, and uncap the sub-RM105 Google campaigns before adding net-new spend. If you want, I can fold this into the /unitar-weekly-report format.   
**ADVERTISE FOR JOHOR TO MALAYSIANS IN SINGAPORE.**

**PERSISTING QUESTIONS:**  
  

WEBSITE TRAFFIC:

  - Yes the website traffic was higher in Apr - June 2025 vs Apr - June 2026

  - Our current marketing efforts are mainly to drive lead volume and quality.
  - Even our TOF campaigns are running to increase brand awareness, thus NO campaign is running to increase website traffic.  
      

RETARGETING:

  - What is prospecting vs retargeting in Unitar’s context?
  - **Prospecting**: To acquire new users and get them INTO the UNITAR ecosystem i.e become a lead
  - **Retargeting**: To target leads that already exist IN the UNITAR ecosystem.  
      
  - Audaura's target is to ACQUIRE NEW LEADS, i.e run prospecting campaigns.  
      
  - We do run campaigns labeled as ‘retargeting’. This is for users who have either **opened** a lead form/visited website/followed social media. THESE USERS ARE STILL NOT LEADS, so we target them to **SUBMIT** a lead form
  - Retargeting: Total of 11%of total adspend (1% of TikTok)

**Our total investment for the week was **RM172,149**, generating **2,415** leads at a blended Cost Per Lead (CPL) of **RM71.28.**

Week on week, spend decreased by **5.7%**, while lead volume increased **+28.9%**, resulting in a **26.7% improvement in blended CPL** - a strong week overall driven primarily by Meta's performance.

Meta remained the efficiency leader, delivering 1,673 leads at a blended RM43.50 CPL. Compared to last week, spend decreased -14%, leads increased +85%, and CPL improved by 40%, making it the primary driver of this week's gains.

Google increased investment (+6% spend) and maintained similar lead volume (593 leads; -2% WoW), with CPL increasing to RM112.90 (+10%). Efficiency softened and remains above the target range.

TikTok saw spend increase +16%, but lead volume declined to 149 (-37%), pushing blended CPL down to RM217 (-5%). While efficiency improved slightly from last week's RM228.15 CPL, overall volume continued to weaken, indicating creative fatigue and limited scaling potential.

Total investment for the week was **RM166,147.56**, generating **1,473.70 leads** at a blended Cost Per Lead (CPL) of **RM112.74**.

Compared to last week (13–19 July), spend fell -7% while leads dropped -34%, pushing blended CPL up +42% (RM79.59 → RM112.74). This is a sharp regression, and it's driven almost entirely by one change on Meta.

Meta's CPL nearly doubled to **RM100.95** (from RM51.64, +95%) as leads fell -50% (1,383 → 688) even though spend only dropped -3%. The cause is structural, not creative fatigue: the Pakistan Instant Form campaign — which drove 754 leads at just RM5.92 CPL last week — was paused this week and is now effectively at zero spend (one residual lead only). Strip Pakistan out of last week's base and the Malaysia-only comparison tells a different story: RM106.44 CPL on 629 leads last week vs. RM100.95 CPL on 688 leads this week (almost entirely Malaysia) — a modest improvement. In short, Meta's blended CPL looks much worse only because a very cheap, high-volume lever got switched off; the core Malaysia campaigns are actually healthy.

Google continued to improve: spend -14%, leads -12%, and CPL edged down to **RM101.70** (from RM103.94, -2%). Still above the RM90s target, but the efficiency trend is now positive for a second straight week.

TikTok held roughly flat on spend (-1%) while leads rose +6% (149 → 158), pulling CPL down to **RM207.95** (from RM222.08, -6%). This breaks the three-week stall in the RM215–228 band, though CPL remains well above target.

The new batch of TikTok ONE creator projects is still pending Unitar's program list. In the meantime, our TikTok account manager has raised a ticket to source more videos from the currently launched programs.

One flag: since Pakistan is now paused, next week's Meta comparison will finally be apples-to-apples (Malaysia vs. Malaysia) — worth watching whether that RM100.95 holds or drifts.

**  
****Insight:****  
**Meta delivered RM69,456.40 spend / 688 leads / RM100.95 blended CPL — CPL nearly doubled from last week's RM51.64 (+95%) as the ultra-cheap Pakistan volume (754 leads at RM5.92) was paused entirely (down to RM0 spend, 1 residual lead). Spend held nearly flat (-3%), so this is a volume/mix shift, not a Malaysia performance problem.

Message Generation weakened further as a lead line (16 leads / RM842.06, down from 27 / RM523.66, CPL +61%) and remains best read only as a conversation source.

  
  

**Campaign Level Performance Insight**

**Insight:****  
**Retargeting kept regressing — 161 leads at RM106.57, down from 191 leads at RM90.05 last week (leads -16%, CPL +18%), now well outside the June RM53–66 range. Main Campus Prospecting also softened (165 leads at RM59.51, from 190 at RM49.71, CPL +20%) but is still the volume anchor. Perkeso enters the top 3 for the first time — cheapest of the three at RM25.25 — filling some of the gap Pakistan's pause left behind.

**Action steps:**

  - Retargeting CPL has now risen two weeks straight (RM90.05 → RM106.57): pause and refresh the weakest ad sets rather than let spend keep flowing to fatigued creative.
  - Lean further into Perkeso (RM25.25 CPL) — it's now the most efficient Malaysia campaign and has room to scale.

**  
****Insight:** Mixed results among the top converters. Low-Intent T1a+T2 kept improving and scaling (90.51 conv / RM71.02, from 75 / RM83.85 — conv +21%, CPL -15%), now the clear efficiency leader. Brand T2 Test reversed hard (54.97 conv / RM104.49, from 70 / RM82.24 — conv -21%, CPL +27%), giving back last week's recovery. Brand T1a takes the \#3 spot this week, pushing Brand T1b out of the top 3; T1b itself held steady-to-softer (33.52 conv / RM91.00, from 47 / RM87.18).

Outside the top three, SPM (T1a EN) stayed the most efficient high-volume campaign (31.90 conv / RM82.49, improved from \~RM88.67), while Brand T2 non-test remained the weakest of the larger campaigns, though its CPL ticked down slightly (27.86 conv / RM160.45, from \~RM170.47).

**Action steps:**

  - Keep shifting budget toward Low-Intent T1a+T2 and SPM — both are compounding efficiency gains week over week.
  - Investigate Brand T2 Test's reversal (CPL +27%) before restoring any budget increases there.
  - Continue monitoring Brand T1b/T1a — one is softening while the other is emerging; worth confirming this isn't just budget reallocation between near-identical audiences.

**Meta**

### **  
****Performance Analysis**

#### **Website Conversion vs. Instant Form Campaigns**

|  |  |  |  |  |  |
| :-: | :-: | :-: | :-: | :-: | :-: |
| \*\*Campaign Type\*\* | \*\*Spend (RM)\*\* | \*\*Leads\*\* | \*\*CPL (RM)\*\* | \*\*Message Conversation\*\* | \*\*Cost per message\*\* |
| \*\*Instant Form\*\* | RM240,493.26 | 5,774 | RM41.65 | 5,019 | RM47.92 |
| \*\*Message Generation\*\* | RM61,746.37 | 133 | RM464.26 | 1,173 | RM52.64 |
| \*\*Awareness/\*\*\*\*  &#10;\*\*\*\*LPV\*\* | RM9,280.31 | / | / | / | / |
| \*\*Website Conversion\*\* | RM12,243.27 | 244 | RM50.18 | 298 | RM41.08 |

**  
****Insight:****  
**Meta delivered RM323,763.21 spend / 6,159 leads / RM52.57 blended CPL with spend +31%, leads +84% and CPL -29% versus June's RM73.68. On headline numbers this is Meta's best month since April (RM46.37), reversing three months of rising cost (Apr RM46 → May RM68 → Jun RM74 → Jul RM53).

Instant Form did the work: RM240,493.26 / 5,774 leads / RM41.65 CPL, with leads +97% and CPL -26% vs June (RM56.21). Almost all of that incremental volume is Pakistan and Perkeso.

Website Conversion is now a healthy, efficient line at RM12,243.27 / 244 leads / RM50.18 which is better than blended Meta and worth continuing.

Message Generation deteriorated on both measures: 133 leads at RM464.26 (June: 150 at RM397.63), and as a conversation driver it is now far more expensive with 1,173 conversations at RM52.64 each, versus 2,815 at RM21.19 in June (volume -58%, cost per message +148%). At RM61,746 it is our third-largest Meta line and no longer earns that spend.

  
  

  

**Campaign Level Performance Insight**

#### **Top 3 Campaigns by Leads:**

|  |  |  |
| :-: | :-: | :-: |
| \*\*Campaign Name\*\* | \*\*Leads\*\* | \*\*CPL (RM)\*\* |
| \*\*Audaura/ FB/ C1 2026/ Scale Up/ Lead Generation/ Instant Form/\*\* \*\*Retargeting\*\*\*\*/ ABO/ CAPI\*\* | 813 | RM94.37 |
| \*\*Audaura/ FB/ C2 2026/ Lead Generation/ LG/ MC/ Prospecting/ ABO/ CAPI\*\* | 791 | RM55.40 |
| \*\*Audaura/ FB/ C3 2026/ Lead Generation/ LG/ MY/\*\* \*\*Perkeso\*\*\*\*/ Prospecting/ ABO/ CAPI\*\* | 518 | RM15.99 |
|   |   |   |
| \*\*Audaura/ FB/ C2 2026/ Lead Generation/ LG/ Pakistan/ Prospecting/ ABO/ CAPI\*\* | 2,881 | RM6.43 |

**Insight:****  
**Pakistan Prospecting dominated the month at 2,881 leads / RM6.43 CPL, up from 322 leads / RM13.26 in June with both far more volume and half the cost. Perkeso launched well at 518 leads / RM15.99 and is now our most efficient Malaysia campaign.

C1 Scale-Up Retargeting fell to 813 leads at RM94.37 (June: 993 at RM53.57) at CPL +76% while volume dropped 18%, and it regressed in every week of July. C2 Main Campus Prospecting held volume (791 leads, flat) but CPL rose 20% to RM55.40, and remains the volume anchor.

**Key Next Steps:**

  - **Get a definitive answer on Pakistan lead quality:**  it now underpins our blended CPL, and Pakistan alone is nearly half of Meta's lead volume. Confirm downstream enrolment intent before we plan August around it. The campaign was paused in the final week of July, so this is now urgent to resolve either way.
  - **Scale Perkeso:**it is the cheapest Malaysia campaign at RM15.99 and stepped into the top 3 as Pakistan wound down. It is the most credible replacement for Pakistan volume. Apply the agreed creative direction (fear-based framing, testimonials, program value, limited-seat scarcity).
  - **Fix or cut Retargeting:**  two consecutive months of decline and a rise from RM53.57 to RM94.37. Pause the fatigued ad sets and refresh creative rather than continuing to fund them.

## **Google****  
  
**

### **Overall Google Summary**

|  |  |  |  |
| :-: | :-: | :-: | :-: |
| \*\*Campaign Type\*\* | \*\*Cost\*\* | \*\*Conversions\*\* | \*\*CPL\*\* |
| Search | RM311,263.71 | 2,883 | RM107.98 |

### **  
****Insight:** Google Search spent RM311,263.71 for 2,883 leads at RM107.98 CPL with spend -13%, leads -22% and CPL +12% versus June's RM96.19. This is Google's weakest month across the last five (Mar RM91.70, Apr RM57.38, May RM88.79, Jun RM96.19), and the third consecutive month of rising CPL. Google also fell from 50% to 40% of total spend — part deliberate reallocation toward Meta, part efficiency loss.

The monthly figure understates where we finished. Weekly CPL improved consistently through July (RM112.90 → RM103.94 → RM101.70), so the damage was concentrated early in the month and the channel is trending back toward the RM90s target.

  

### **Top 3 Campaigns by Conversions:**

|  |  |  |  |
| :-: | :-: | :-: | :-: |
| \*\*Campaign\*\* | \*\*Cost (RM)\*\* | \*\*Conv.\*\* | \*\*CPL (RM)\*\* |
| Aud\\\_Unitar\\\_MY\\\_SEM\\\_Level of study\\\_Low intent\\\_T1a+T2\\\_EN+BM\\\_Max\\\_Conversion\\\_TCPA | RM27,358.36 | 349 | RM78.45 |
| Aud\\\_Unitar\\\_MY\\\_SEM\\\_Brand\\\_T2\\\_Max\\\_Conversion\\\_TCPA\\\_Test | RM25,633.07 | 249 | RM102.90 |
| Aud\\\_Unitar\\\_MY\\\_SEM\\\_Brand\\\_T1a\\\_Max\\\_Conversion\\\_TCPA | RM25,688.39 | 219 | RM117.41 |

**Insight:** Low-Intent T1a+T2 was the only top converter with improvement at 349 conversions at RM78.45, up from 338 at RM81 in June. It is now clearly our most efficient volume campaign (finishing at RM71.02).

The Brand campaigns are the source of Google's regression. Brand T2 Test fell to 249 conversions at RM102.90 (June: 315 at RM76: conversions -21%, CPL +35%), and Brand T1a to 219 at RM117.41 (June: 265 at RM91 — CPL +29%). Brand was our efficiency backbone in June and is now our most expensive volume.

Outside the top three, SPM remained the most efficient high-volume campaign (finishing July around RM82), while Brand T2 (non-test) stayed the weakest of the larger campaigns at roughly RM160.

**Key Next Steps:**

  - **Shift budget toward Low-Intent T1a+T2 and SPM:** both compounded efficiency gains every week in July and are the clearest place to add spend.
  - **Run a proper diagnosis on the Brand deterioration:** a 29–35% CPL rise across two campaigns points to something systemic (auction competition, CPC inflation, or landing-page/quality-score) rather than campaign-level noise. Diagnose before restoring the budget.
  - **Restructure or cut Brand T2 non-test:**  it is consuming a meaningful budget at roughly double the account's best CPL.
  - **Hold the recovery:**  it exited July at RM101.70 and improved; protect that trajectory rather than re-scaling spend into it prematurely.

### **Performance Analysis**

**Top 5 Campaigns by Conversions:**

|  |  |  |  |
| :-: | :-: | :-: | :-: |
| \*\*Campaign\*\* | \*\*Cost (RM)\*\* | \*\*Conv.\*\* | \*\*CPL (RM)\*\* |
| audaura\\\_tiktok\\\_MOF\\\_InstantForms\\\_ConversionLeads\\\_A1\\\_tt\\\_ONE\\\_ABO | RM63,723.12 | 496 | RM128.47 |
| audaura\\\_tiktok\\\_MOF\\\_InstantForms\\\_ConversionLeads\\\_A1\\\_Masters\\\_CBO | RM26,538.86 | 114 | RM232.80 |
| audaura\\\_tiktok\\\_MOF\\\_InstantForms\\\_ConversionLeads\\\_A1\\\_T1a\\\_CBO | RM7,486.041 | 13 | RM575.85  |

  

**Insight:**TikTok delivered RM146,136.70 spend / 640 leads / RM228.34 blended CPL — spend +30% but leads only +8%, so CPL worsened 20% from June's RM189.65. We spent RM34,000 more to buy 49 more leads. This is TikTok's worst month since March (RM346), undoing the gains made in April and May (RM126 → RM206 → RM190 → RM228).

TOF Awareness is the single biggest drag: RM48,388.68 with no direct leads, up 50% from June's RM32,287 and now 33% of all TikTok spend. Excluding it, TikTok's lead-generating spend ran at roughly RM153 per lead — still above target, but a very different number from RM228.

MOF Instant Forms weakened to RM156.90 CPL (June: RM136.64). Within it, the ONE creative remains the engine (496 conversions) but its efficiency eroded as we scaled — RM128.47 versus RM104.13 in June (+23%). Masters worsened to RM232.80 (+22%) and T1a stayed unviable at RM575.85 on 13 conversions.

One positive: the final week of July broke the three-week stall, with blended CPL down to RM207.95 and the ONE creative recovering to RM120.38 — evidence that pulling spend back improves efficiency rather than scaling it.

  

**Key Next Steps:**

  - **Stop scaling A1\_tt\_ONE\_ABO into higher CPL:**  across June and July the pattern is consistent: every time we scale it, CPL rises; when we pull back, it recovers. Cap it at the level where it converts nearer RM100 until we have more creative.
  - **Cut or rebuild A1\_T1a\_CBO:** it has been above RM500 CPL for two consecutive months on negligible volume. There is no case for continuing it in its current form.
  - **Unblock the creative pipeline:** TikTok ONE video generation remains the binding constraint (only 2 new videos received). The programs list from Unitar is the blocker for the 3 prepared project drafts; without it, TikTok cannot improve regardless of budget.
